=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a tree with N \times M + 1 vertices numbered 0, 1, \dots, N \times M. The i-th edge (1 \leq i \leq N \times M) connects vertices i and \max(i - N, 0).
Vertex 0 is painted. The other vertices are unpainted.
Takahashi is at vertex 0. As long as there exists an unpainted vertex, he performs the following operation:

- He chooses one of the vertices adjacent to his current vertex uniformly at random (all choices are independent) and moves to that vertex. Then, if the vertex he is on is unpainted, he paints it.

Find the expected number of times he performs the operation, modulo 998244353.

What is the expected value modulo 998244353?

It can be proved that the sought expected value is always rational. Under the constraints of this problem, when that value is expressed as an irreducible fraction \frac{P}{Q}, it can also be proved that Q \not\equiv 0 \pmod{998244353}. Then, there uniquely exists an integer R such that R \times Q \equiv P \pmod{998244353}, 0 \leq R \lt 998244353. Report this R.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M

Output

Print the expected number of times he performs the operation, modulo 998244353.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq M \leq 2 \times 10^5
- N and M are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 2

Sample Output 1

20

For example, Takahashi could behave as follows.

- Moves to vertex 1 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 0. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 1. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 3 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 1. This action is chosen with probability 1.
- Moves to vertex 0. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 2 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.
- Moves to vertex 4 and paints it. This action is chosen with probability \frac{1}{2}.

He behaves in this way with probability \frac{1}{128}, in which case the number of operations is 8. The expected number of operations is 20.

Sample Input 2

123456 185185

Sample Output 2

69292914

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now we can set up recurrences.

Let E_k = expected remaining time from root with k unvisited leaves (k=0..N). E_0=0.

Let U_k = expected remaining time when at depth1 of an unvisited chain, with k unvisited including current (k>=1). At depth1, random walk until either hit leaf (success) or root (failure). If success:
- time from depth1 to leaf conditional mean a.
- after painting leaf, if k=1, stop (remaining 0).
- if k>1, we are at leaf of visited chain, with k-1 unvisited elsewhere. Need expected remaining time from leaf to complete. Let's call G_{k-1} = expected remaining time from leaf of a visited chain when there are j=k-1 unvisited chains. As before, for j>0, G_j = h_M + E_j = M^2 + E_j. For j=0, G_0=0 (stop immediately after painting last leaf; no time from leaf to root).
If failure:
- time from depth1 to root conditional mean b.
- then at root with k unvisited, E_k.

Thus:
U_k = p*( a + (0 if k==1 else G_{k-1}) ) + (1-p)*( b + E_k ).

Where p=1/M, a=(M^2-1)/3, b=(2M-1)/3, h_M=M^2.

At root:
E_k = expected remaining time from root. At root, choose neighbor uniformly among N.
If choose visited chain (prob (N-k)/N): move to depth1 (1 step), then from depth1 visited to root expected h_1 = 2M-1, then E_k. So contribution (N-k)/N * (1 + h_1 + E_k) = (N-k)/N * (2M + E_k).
If choose unvisited chain (prob k/N): move to depth1 (1 step), then U_k. Contribution k/N * (1 + U_k).
Thus:
E_k = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(h_1 + E_k)? Wait the "1" for initial step? Let's write:
E_k = (k/N)*(1 + U_k) + ((N-k)/N)*(1 + h_1 + E_k).
= 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(h_1 + E_k).
Since h_1=2M-1, 1+h_1=2M.
Alternatively E_k = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(2M-1 + E_k).
Let's keep.

We can solve for E_k in terms of U_k:
E_k = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(2M-1 + E_k).
Bring E_k term:
E_k [1 - (N-k)/N] = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(2M-1)
=> (k/N) E_k = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(2M-1)
=> E_k = (N/k) + U_k + ((N-k)/k)(2M-1).
Alternatively E_k = U_k + N/k + (N-k)(2M-1)/k.
Simplify E_k = U_k + [N + (N-k)(2M-1)]/k.
But maybe better.

Now U_k formula:
U_k = p*a + p*(0 if k==1 else G_{k-1}) + (1-p)*b + (1-p)*E_k.
Let C = p*a + (1-p)*b. Compute:
p*a = (1/M)*((M^2-1)/3) = (M^2-1)/(3M).
(1-p)*b = ((M-1)/M)*((2M-1)/3) = (M-1)(2M-1)/(3M).
Sum = [M^2 -1 + (M-1)(2M-1)]/(3M) = (M^2 -1 + 2M^2 -3M +1)/(3M) = (3M^2 -3M)/(3M) = M -1.
So C = M -1. Nice! Unconditional expected time from depth1 to absorption (root or leaf) is M-1 (e_1), indeed.

Then U_k = (M-1) + (1-p) E_k + p * S_{k-1}, where S_{k-1} = 0 if k=1 else G_{k-1} = M^2 + E_{k-1} if k>1.
Thus:
For k=1:
U_1 = (M-1) + (1-p) E_1. (Since success final no G)
For k>=2:
U_k = (M-1) + (1-p) E_k + (1/M)*(M^2 + E_{k-1})
= (M-1) + M + (1/M) E_{k-1} + ((M-1)/M) E_k
= (2M-1) + ((M-1)/M) E_k + (1/M) E_{k-1}.
Because (1/M)*M^2 = M.

Let's verify with sample N=2,M=2.

M=2: p=1/2, a=1, b=1, C=1=M-1.
Equations:
E_1? Use root formula with k=1:
E_1 = 1 + (1/2) U_1 + (1/2)(h_1 + E_1) where h_1=3? Wait M=2 h_1=2M-1=3. So E_1 = 1 + 0.5 U_1 +0.5(3+E_1) = 1+0.5U_1+1.5+0.5E_1 => 0.5E_1=2.5+0.5U_1 => E_1=5+U_1.
U_1 = C + (1-p)E_1 =1 +0.5E_1.
So E_1=5+1+0.5E_1 =>0.5E_1=6 => E_1=12. Is expected time to paint one leaf in 2-chain starting root? Let's compute maybe 12? Let's test: root has two chains, one unvisited one visited. At root, choose unvisited with prob1/2, visited with prob1/2. If visited, excursion mean 4 then root. If unvisited, from depth1, success prob1/2, failure prob1/2. Could be 12. Seems plausible.

For k=2:
E_2 = 1 + (2/2) U_2 +0? Actually N-k=0, so E_2 = 1 + U_2. (Only unvisited chains)
U_2 = (2M-1)=3 + ((M-1)/M)E_2 + (1/M)E_1 = 3 +0.5E_2+0.5E_1.
E_2=1+U_2 =4+0.5E_2+0.5E_1 =>0.5E_2=4+0.5E_1 => E_2=8+E_1=20. Sample output 20. Great.

So recurrences work.

Need solve for E_N modulo MOD for N,M up to 2e5. Recurrence is first-order? Let's derive closed form.

We have E_k in terms of U_k:
E_k = U_k + A_k, where A_k = N/k + (N-k)(2M-1)/k? Let's simplify.

From root:
E_k = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(2M-1 + E_k)
=> (k/N) E_k = 1 + (k/N) U_k + ((N-k)/N)(2M-1)
=> E_k = U_k + N/k + ((N-k)(2M-1))/k.
Let D_k = N/k + ((N-k)(2M-1))/k = [N + (N-k)(2M-1)]/k = [N(2M) - k(2M-1)]/k? Compute N + (N-k)(2M-1) = N + N(2M-1) - k(2M-1) = 2MN - k(2M-1). So D_k = 2MN/k - (2M-1). Thus:
E_k = U_k + 2MN/k - (2M-1).   (Eq R)

For k=1:
U_1 = (M-1) + ((M-1)/M) E_1.
Then E_1 = U_1 + 2MN - (2M-1) = (M-1) + ((M-1)/M)E_1 + 2MN - 2M +1
= 2MN - M +? Let's compute (M-1) -2M +1 = -M. So E_1 = 2MN - M + ((M-1)/M)E_1.
=> E_1/M = 2MN - M? Wait E_1 - ((M-1)/M)E_1 = (1/M)E_1 = 2MN - M = M(2N-1). Thus E_1 = M^2(2N-1). Check sample N=2,M=2: E_1=4*3=12 yes. Good.

For k>=2:
U_k = (2M-1) + ((M-1)/M) E_k + (1/M) E_{k-1}.
Then E_k = U_k + D_k
= (2M-1) + ((M-1)/M)E_k + (1/M)E_{k-1} + 2MN/k - (2M-1)
= ((M-1)/M)E_k + (1/M)E_{k-1} + 2MN/k.
Thus (1/M) E_k = (1/M) E_{k-1} + 2MN/k
=> E_k = E_{k-1} + 2M^2 N / k.

Wow simple recurrence! For k>=2:
E_k = E_{k-1} + 2 M^2 N / k.
Check sample N=2,M=2: E_1=12, E_2=12 + 2*4*2/2=12+8=20. Good.

Then for general N:
E_N = E_1 + sum_{k=2}^N 2 M^2 N / k = M^2(2N-1) + 2 M^2 N * (H_N - 1)
= M^2 [ 2N -1 + 2N(H_N -1) ]
= M^2 [ 2N H_N - 1 ]? Since 2N -1 +2N H_N -2N = 2N H_N -1. Yes.
So expected value = M^2 ( 2N H_N - 1 ), where H_N = sum_{i=1}^N 1/i.

MOD=998244353
inv = [0]*(N+1)
if N>=1: inv[1]=1

Let's double-check derivation of recurrence and formula with a more formal approach to ensure no hidden issue: The expected number of operations until all vertices painted. We considered leaves as only unvisited? Actually all vertices along chain are unpainted initially. When first entering an unvisited chain, you may paint intermediate vertices before leaf. Does our "success" event of hitting leaf before root imply all vertices in that chain become painted? Not necessarily. Suppose you enter chain, go down to depth d<M, then return to root without hitting leaf. You may have painted some prefix of the chain. Later when you enter the same chain again, it is still "unvisited" in terms of leaf not painted, but some vertices are already painted. Does this affect the process? The operation only paints unpainted vertices; if you revisit a painted vertex, no new paint. The random walk transition probabilities are unaffected by painted status. The goal is to paint all vertices. We modeled "unvisited chain" as leaf not visited; if leaf not visited, the chain is not fully painted. But if some prefix painted, when you later enter the chain, you might not paint those prefix vertices again, but that doesn't change transition probabilities or time. The event of eventually hitting leaf before root starting from depth1 is independent of painted status. However, if you previously painted some vertices, when you enter the chain at depth1, the vertex at depth1 is already painted, but the operation still counts the move. Our model counts every move regardless of painting. So painted status of internal vertices doesn't affect future except leaf count? Wait, if you enter an unvisited chain and go down to depth d<M then return to root without hitting leaf, you have painted vertices 1..d (or some subset). Later, when you enter the same chain and go down, you may pass through painted vertices; no new paint until you go beyond previous max depth. The first time you hit leaf, all vertices on path become painted (because to reach leaf you traverse all depths). So leaf visited iff all vertices in chain painted. Thus tracking leaf visited is sufficient for completion. The random walk behavior is independent of painted status. So our "attempt" on an unvisited chain from root: starting at depth1, if it hits leaf before root, then all vertices in chain become painted (leaf painted) and success. If it returns to root before leaf, no leaf painted; some prefix may be painted but chain remains unvisited. The probability p of hitting leaf before root from depth1 is same every time, because the walk starts at depth1 each time (after returning to root and choosing chain). The fact that some prefix is already painted doesn't change p. The expected time from depth1 to leaf/root conditional also same. So our excursion model is valid. Good.

At root with k unpainted arms:
- With probability (N-k)/N, choose a painted arm. The walk goes to its first vertex (1 step), then eventually returns to root. Expected time to return to root from depth 1 is h_1 = 2M-1. Then state root with k unpainted. So contribution: (N-k)/N * (1 + h_1 + f_k) = (N-k)/N * (2M + f_k).
- With probability k/N, choose unpainted arm. Move to depth1 (1 step), then state g_k. Contribution: k/N * (1 + g_k).
Thus:
f_k = (N-k)/N * (2M + f_k) + k/N * (1 + g_k).
Multiply N:
N f_k = (N-k)(2M + f_k) + k(1+g_k)
= (N-k)2M + (N-k)f_k + k + k g_k
=> k f_k = 2M(N-k) + k + k g_k
=> f_k = g_k + 2M(N-k)/k + 1 = g_k + 2MN/k - 2M + 1? Wait earlier D_k = 2MN/k - (2M-1) = 2MN/k -2M +1. Yes.

Check: 2M(N-k)/k +1 = 2MN/k -2M +1. Good.

Now g_k: At depth1 of unpainted arm, with k unpainted arms including current. The random walk on the arm until it either reaches leaf (depth M) or root (depth0). Let:
p = P(reach leaf before root) = 1/M.
A = E[time to reach leaf | reach leaf before root] = (M^2-1)/3.
B = E[time to reach root | reach root before leaf] = (2M-1)/3.
If reaches leaf:
- If k=1, all arms painted, stop. Additional time after reaching leaf = 0.
- If k>1, the arm becomes painted; we are at leaf. Need expected time to finish from leaf with k-1 unpainted arms. From leaf, expected time to return to root is h_M = M^2. Then at root with k-1 unpainted, expected f_{k-1}. So additional = M^2 + f_{k-1}.
If reaches root:
- Arm still unpainted, state root with k unpainted: additional f_k.
Thus:
g_k = p A + (1-p) B + (1-p) f_k + p * (0 if k=1 else M^2 + f_{k-1}).

Compute pA + (1-p)B = M-1 as above. Also p*M^2 = M. So:
For k=1:
g_1 = M-1 + (1-p) f_1.
For k>=2:
g_k = M-1 + (1-p) f_k + M + (1/M) f_{k-1}? Wait p*(M^2+f_{k-1}) = p M^2 + p f_{k-1} = M + (1/M) f_{k-1}. So g_k = (M-1) + M + (1-p)f_k + (1/M)f_{k-1} = 2M-1 + ((M-1)/M)f_k + (1/M)f_{k-1}. Yes.

Now combine with f_k = g_k + 2MN/k -2M +1.

For k=1:
f_1 = [M-1 + ((M-1)/M)f_1] + 2MN -2M +1
= ((M-1)/M)f_1 + 2MN - M.
Then (1/M)f_1 = 2MN - M => f_1 = M^2(2N-1). Good.

For k>=2:
f_k = [2M-1 + ((M-1)/M)f_k + (1/M)f_{k-1}] + 2MN/k -2M +1
= ((M-1)/M)f_k + (1/M)f_{k-1} + 2MN/k.
Then (1/M)f_k = (1/M)f_{k-1} + 2MN/k => f_k = f_{k-1} + 2M^2 N/k.
Thus f_N = M^2(2N-1) + 2M^2 N (H_N -1) = M^2(2N H_N -1). Correct.

Now, code details:
```
import sys
MOD=998244353
def main():
    N,M=map(int,sys.stdin.readline().split())
    inv=[0]*(N+1)
    if N>=1:
        inv[1]=1
    for i in range(2,N+1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD//i) * inv[MOD%i] % MOD
    H=0
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        H += inv[i]
        if H >= MOD: H -= MOD? But sum of up to 2e5 terms each <MOD, H can exceed MOD multiple times if not mod each step. Use H = (H + inv[i]) % MOD. Or if H>=MOD subtract once each step since H<MOD and inv<MOD, sum<2MOD, so subtract once enough. Do:
        H += inv[i]
        if H >= MOD: H -= MOD
    ans = (M % MOD) * (M % MOD) % MOD
    factor = (2 * N % MOD * H % MOD - 1) % MOD
    ans = ans * factor % MOD
    print(ans)
if __name__=="__main__": main()
```

Now, what about an arm that is partially painted but leaf not painted: We classify as unpainted. When we enter it, the first vertex may already be painted, but the move from root to depth1 is counted. Our g_k starts at depth1 after that move. The expected time to hit leaf or root from depth1 is independent of painted status. However, if some internal vertices are painted, when we pass them no painting occurs, but operation count still counts moves. The random walk transition probabilities are same. So g_k unaffected. The only thing: If the arm is partially painted and we reach root before leaf, no new leaf; if we reach leaf, all remaining unpainted vertices in that arm get painted (because to reach leaf we traverse all depths; any previously unpainted vertices on path get painted). Thus success criterion leaf hit remains correct. Good.

Let's write robust input:
```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    N, M = map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.readline().split())
    inv = [0] * (N + 1)
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
    h = 0
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        h += inv[i]
        if h >= MOD:
            h -= MOD
    m = M % MOD
    ans = m * m % MOD
    ans = ans * ((2 * N % MOD) * h % MOD - 1) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's think about modular inverse array memory. If N=200000, inv list of Python ints ~5.6 MB, fine. Could compute H with recurrence for inverses and sum in one loop? We need inv[MOD % i] for current i, which is previous. We can compute and accumulate in one loop:
```
inv = [0]*(N+1)
inv[1]=1
h=1
for i in range(2,N+1):
    inv[i] = MOD - (MOD//i) * inv[MOD%i] % MOD
    h += inv[i]
    if h >= MOD: h -= MOD
```
For N=1, h=1. This avoids second loop. But need ensure inv[1] set. We'll do this.

Code:
```
import sys
MOD=998244353
def main():
    N,M = map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split())
    inv=[0]*(N+1)
    h=0
    if N>=1:
        inv[1]=1
        h=1
    for i in range(2,N+1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
        h += inv[i]
        if h >= MOD:
            h -= MOD
    m = M % MOD
    ans = m * m % MOD
    ans = ans * ((2 * N % MOD) * h % MOD - 1) % MOD
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
Check h addition with one subtraction: h<MOD, inv[i]<MOD, sum<2MOD, one subtraction enough. Good.

If N=0 not possible. If N=1, inv[1]=1, h=1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with Python's `%` for negative in factor:
`factor = (2 * N % MOD) * h % MOD
ans = m * m % MOD * (factor - 1) % MOD`

Now, let's write final code cleanly:
```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0]); M = int(data[1])

    inv = [0] * (N + 1)
    h = 0
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
        h = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
        h += inv[i]
        if h >= MOD:
            h -= MOD

    m = M % MOD
    ans = m * m % MOD
    ans = ans * ((2 * N % MOD) * h % MOD - 1) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that the expected value formula should be `M^2 * (2*N*H_N
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    N = int(data[0])
    M = int(data[1])

    inv = [0] * (N + 1)
    h = 0
    if N >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
        h = 1

    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD
        h += inv[i]
        if h >= MOD:
            h -= MOD

    m = M % MOD
    ans = m * m % MOD
    ans = ans * ((2 * N % MOD) * h % MOD - 1) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
